--- PROD-15 Combined Heuristic Intro --- --- Three-Pronged Engineering Synthesis — Carrington Storm Motors --- Document: PROD-15 Stage 2 Polymer Flow & Separation Synthesis: Williams (Physics) + Keymaker (Architecture) + El Segundo (Operations) Author: CSM Fabrication Division | Date: 2026-11-01

--- COMBINED HEURISTIC: PROD-15 STAGE 2 POLYMER FLOW & SEPARATION --- The Challenge: Separate 75 kg of mixed molten polymer per batch by density at 150-450°C, in an all-dielectric vessel, with PVC HCl management, delivering two pure fractions to downstream molding — every 35 minutes, 8,000 hours/year. [Pause]

[Pause: Long]

--- I. [Pause] PHYSICS VERDICT (Williams): IT WORKS --- Density is our friend. [Pause] The 44% density gap between polyolefins (0.76) and engineering polymers (1.35) at melt state is enormous. [Pause] At 12.6 G (150 RPM, r=250 mm), the centrifugal potential Φ = 7.7 J/kg provides 13.9 kJ/kg stratification energy over 30 minutes. [Pause] Modified Stokes' law in shear-thinning melt gives 1.7 µm/s settling for 1mm PET sphere — 3 mm travel in dwell time, sufficient for annular layer formation. [Pause] Rheology helps, not hurts. [Pause] Carreau-Yasuda shear-thinning drops effective viscosity from 150 Pa·s (zero-shear) to 8 Pa·s at bowl wall shear rate (3,925 s⁻¹). [Pause] This 19× reduction accelerates stratification and reduces centrifuge power. [Pause] Thermal window is tight but usable. [Pause] PVC dehydrochlorination at 210°C is the only hard limit. [Pause] NaHCO₃ in-drum injection at 2× stoichiometric with POF HCl feedback control (50 ppm hold threshold) contains it. [Pause] All other polymers stable to 300-350°C; PEEK/PTFE to 400°C+. [Pause] Our 450°C peak is for char formation, not polymer. [Pause] Energy is favorable. [Pause] Net 432 kWh/ton polymer recovered vs 3,500 kWh/ton virgin production = 88% embodied energy recovery. [Pause] Heat recovery from mold cooling (8 kWh), vacuum exhaust (2 kWh), bearing cooling (1 kWh) closes the loop. [Pause] Williams Bottom Line: The physics is sound. [Pause] Density separation at 12.6 G in shear-thinning melt works. [Pause] PVC is manageable. [Pause] Energy balance is excellent. [Pause]

[Pause: Long]

--- II. [Pause] ARCHITECTURE VERDICT (Keymaker): INTEGRATED BUT BOTTLENECKED --- Interface contracts are clean. [Pause] Stage 2 receives <1% moisture feed at 150°C from PROD-14, discharges 250-280°C melt to PROD-08 mold carousel, sends char/filler to PROD-16 pyrolysis, HCl salts to PROD-07 gas system, oligomers to Stage 1 water recovery. [Pause] All-dielectric constraint satisfied: BFRP bowl, ZrO₂ liner, Si₃N₄ bearings, PEEK CF40 seals, ultrasonic drive, POF sensors. [Pause] Cross-cutting concerns addressed. [Pause] Thermal integration with N₂ pre-heat from Stage 1 exhaust. [Pause] Control system on existing KNbO₃-BaTiO₃ ASIC/POF bus. [Pause] Gas system shares N₂/vacuum/scrubber with Stages 1,3-5. [Pause] Material BOM draws from PROD-12 qualified parts. [Pause] The bottleneck is real. [Pause] Stage 2 at 30 min for 75 kg polymer = 2.5 kg/min. [Pause] Stage 0 (10 min), Stage 1 (20 min), Stage 3 (45 min), Stage 4 (30 min) all process more mass per minute. [Pause] Stage 2 is the pace-setter. [Pause] Scaling path exists. [Pause] Dual centrifuge on same drum (split bowl) doubles throughput. [Pause] 200 RPM (22.3 G) increases G 2.5× for lighter fractions. [Pause] Both are Forge II upgrades — Forge I stays single-centrifuge. [Pause] Keymaker Bottom Line: Architecture integrates cleanly. [Pause] Stage 2 IS the throughput bottleneck. [Pause] Accept for Forge I; design dual-centrifuge for Forge II. [Pause]

[Pause: Long]

--- III. [Pause] OPERATIONS VERDICT (El Segundo): MAINTAINABLE WITH DISCIPLINE --- Consumables drive schedule. [Pause] Candle filters (2,500 hr MTBF) and NaHCO₃ feeder screws (3,000 hr) are the weekly/monthly tasks. [Pause] Designed for 2-hr filter swap, 30-min feeder swap — no bowl entry. [Pause] ZrO₂ liner (5,000 hr) and bearings (10,000 hr) are annual. [Pause] Maintenance windows fit production. [Pause] 20 batches/day = 1 batch/72 min. [Pause] Daily checks in 15 min between batches. [Pause] Weekly filter/feeder swap in 2.5 hr (planned downtime). [Pause] Monthly boroscope/calibration in 1 hr. [Pause] Annual relining in 8 hr (scheduled outage). [Pause] Operator decision at 5 min: resume or abort. [Pause] Post-mortem feeds FOLLOW-18 char protocol. [Pause] Spares budget is lean. [Pause] $59k/yr for 95% availability. [Pause] Critical spares: 4 filter packs, 6 feeder screws, 2 liner sets, 4 actuators. [Pause] All lead times <8 weeks. [Pause] El Segundo Bottom Line: Designed for the 3 AM tech. [Pause] Filters and feeder accessible externally. [Pause] Sensors optical (no hot-zone electronics). [Pause] Emergency sequences automated. [Pause] Spares affordable. [Pause] But — ZrO₂ liner crack is single-point-of-failure requiring 8 hr repair. [Pause] Mitigation: dual liner sets on-site, practiced 4-hr swap. [Pause]

Product Document 16

Williams Heuristic

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — Williams Heuristic Intro ---

Constraint, Necessity, Commitment

Constraint (The Lock)

Thermodynamic necessity forces this design. [Pause] Every thermosetting polymer — epoxy, phenolic, polyester, polyurethane, rubber — has a discrete decomposition temperature window (350-550°C) where covalent bonds break irreversibly. [Pause] You cannot melt a thermoset; you can only pyrolyze it. [Pause] The carbon skeleton remains as char. [Pause] The volatiles leave as gas. [Pause] This is not a choice — it is the physics of crosslinked networks. [Pause] The cascade must pass through 450-700°C to liberate the metals trapped in thermoset matrices (FR4 PCBs, rubber seals, phenolic moldings). [Pause] Skip this window, and the metals stay locked in carbon. [Pause] Rush this window, and you get tar-contaminated metal fractions. [Pause] The 30-minute dwell at 5°C/min ramp through 450-550°C is the minimum time for epoxy/FR4 (the dominant thermoset mass) to complete decomposition. [Pause] This is the lock: thermodynamics sets the schedule, not engineering convenience. [Pause]

Necessity (The Key)

The elemental extraction gap: Modern waste streams (e-waste, auto shredder residue, municipal plastics) contain 15-30% thermosets by weight. [Pause] These thermosets encapsulate the critical metals — copper traces in FR4, nickel in rubber gaskets, cobalt in phenolic resins. [Pause] Mechanical shredding (PROD-11) liberates particles but cannot break the thermoset-metal bond. [Pause] Chemical solvents fail at scale and create secondary waste. [Pause] Thermal decomposition is the only universal key that opens all thermoset locks simultaneously. [Pause] The pyrolysis gas (CO, H₂, CH₄) is not waste — it is the energy carrier that powers the cascade. [Pause] The char is not residue — it is the activated carbon that closes the loop on Stage 1 organics and the reductant that enables Stage 4 metal melting. [Pause] This stage transforms a liability (mixed thermoset waste) into two assets (energy gas + functional char). [Pause] The necessity is absolute: without Stage 3, the cascade stops at plastic. [Pause]

Commitment (The Turn)

This document commits the Aegis-Forge I to three non-negotiables: [Pause] Zero-metal pyrolysis zone: The drum, liners, ports, and gas path from 450-700°C are entirely ceramic and composite (ZrB₂-SiC, Si₃N₄, ZrO₂, BFRP/Elium®). [Pause] No steel, no nickel alloys, no copper. [Pause] Any metal in the hot zone becomes a GIC antenna and a catalytic surface for dioxin formation. [Pause] The dielectric citadel extends through the pyrolysis window. [Pause] [Pause] Char activation integration: The char will not leave Stage 3 cold. [Pause] It exits at 650°C and enters the in-situ activation reactor (steam/CO₂, 850°C, 30 min) using its own sensible heat. [Pause] This commits the design to a coupled reactor system — Stage 3 and activation are one thermodynamic unit. [Pause] Separate them, and you lose 60% of the activation energy to reheat. [Pause] [Pause] REE partitioning fidelity: The N₂/CO₂ atmosphere (<2% O₂) is mandatory, not optional. [Pause] Oxidation at this temperature would convert Nd/Fe/B and Sm/Co into oxides that report to slag instead of metal. [Pause] The cascade's economic engine — separated magnet REE stream (Stage 7) and phosphor REE stream (Stage 8) — depends on this redox discipline. [Pause] One percent O₂ leakage changes the partition coefficient by two orders of magnitude. [Pause] The turn: Measure → Heat → Separate → Verify. [Pause] The control system (Section 13) enforces this at 1 Hz: FBG temperature, mass spec gas composition, char discharge rate. [Pause] If the proxies deviate, the ramp holds. [Pause] The physics cannot be negotiated — only respected.

Keymaker Heuristic

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — Keymaker Heuristic Intro ---

Lock, Key, Turn

The Empirical Lock (Fabrication Fidelity Gap)

Pyrolysis is where theoretical yield meets fabrication reality. [Pause] In the lab, TGA shows epoxy char yield at 22%. [Pause] In the forge, with 500 kg of mixed FR4, rubber, phenolic, and cellulosic feed — varying moisture, filler loading, particle size, halogen content — the actual char yield swings 15-30%. [Pause] The gas composition varies daily. [Pause] The tar load depends on yesterday's shredder blade wear. [Pause] The activation reactor sees char with BET from 300 to 600 m²/g. [Pause] The lock is variability: How do you guarantee 800 m²/g activated carbon from feedstock that refuses to be consistent? [Pause] The fabrication fidelity gap has three faces: [Pause] Feedstock heterogeneity — Category A vs D changes thermoset mass by 4×, filler by 3× [Pause] Kinetic coupling — Epoxy (slow, high Ea) vs phenolic (fast, high char) vs cellulosic (early, low char) all decompose simultaneously [Pause] Product specification tension — Stage 1 needs >800 m²/g BET; Stage 4 needs >85% fixed carbon; external markets need consistent iodine number. [Pause] One char, three masters. [Pause]

The Mathematical Key (Single Computational Primitive)

The distributed activation energy model (DAEM) with real-time mass spec feedback. [Pause] The DAEM reduces the infinite complexity of mixed thermoset pyrolysis to a single state variable: the conversion integral [Equation: \alpha(t) = \int f(E_a) [1 - \exp(-k(E_a)t)] dE_a]. [Pause] The Gaussian distribution [Equation: f(E_a)] is calibrated once per feedstock category (A-D) using lab TGA at 5 heating rates. [Pause] The pre-exponential [Equation: A] and mean [Equation: E_a] become category constants. [Pause] The real-time key: Online mass spectrometer measures CO/CO₂ ratio at 1 Hz. [Pause] This ratio is monotonically related to [Equation: \alpha(t)] for the dominant epoxy fraction. [Pause] The control system inverts the DAEM:

[Equation: \alpha_{\text{measured}}(t) \leftarrow \text{CO/CO}_2 \text{ ratio} \rightarrow \text{adjust } T_{\text{setpoint}}(t) \text{ to track } \alpha_{\text{target}}(t)]

This closes the loop between first-principles kinetics (DAEM) and empirical observation (MS). [Pause] No lookup tables. [Pause] No operator tuning. [Pause] The ramp rate self-adjusts: if CO/CO₂ decays slower than predicted, the ramp slows; if faster, it accelerates. [Pause] The 5°C/min nominal becomes a reference trajectory, not a rigid schedule. [Pause] Activation reactor key: The same DAEM principle applies. [Pause] Burn-off [Equation: X(t)] tracked by bed temperature rise rate ([Equation: dT/dt \propto \text{gasification rate}]). [Pause] Steam/CO₂ flow ratio adjusted to hold [Equation: dT/dt] at target corresponding to 35% burn-off at 30 min. [Pause]

The Turn (Both Directions)

Theory → History (Validation): The DAEM predicts char yield, gas composition, and tar evolution for any feedstock category. [Pause] History validates: Run Category A batch → measure actual char yield, gas GC, tar gravimetric → update [Equation: f(E_a)] Gaussian parameters → next Category A batch runs tighter. [Pause] The model learns the feedstock. [Pause] After 20 batches per category, prediction error <5%. [Pause] History → Theory (Discovery): The mass spec time-series reveals kinetic fingerprints not in literature. [Pause] Example: A secondary CO peak at 620°C (not in TGA) traced to CaCO₃-catalyzed char gasification (Boudouard) from filler decomposition. [Pause] This discovery → added catalytic term to DAEM → now predicts filler-rich feedstock (Category B) accurately. [Pause] The forge teaches the model. [Pause] The turn completes: The computational primitive (DAEM + MS inversion) is the same code running in design simulation (FOLLOW-23 exergy analysis), commissioning (PROD-39 FAT), and daily operation (PROD-23 control). [Pause] One primitive, three regimes. [Pause] The lock (variability) yields to the key (real-time kinetic tracking). [Pause] The turn confirms: theory predicts history, history refines theory. [Pause] The forge knows its own pyrolysis.

El Segundo Heuristic

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — El Segundo Heuristic Intro ---

Mirror, Participation, Protocol

--- Mirror (Self-Reflection: How the Forge Observes Its Own Separation) --- The forge watches its own pyrolysis through 384 fiber Bragg gratings and a mass spectrometer. [Pause] This is not monitoring — this is witnessing. [Pause] Every 1 Hz cycle, the FBG array returns a thermal tomography of the drum wall: 8 zones × 48 sensors, median-filtered, outlier-rejected. [Pause] The mass spectrometer returns a chemical tomography of the gas: 50 m/z channels, calibrated, baseline-corrected. [Pause] Together, they form a mirror that reflects the internal state of the pyrolysis back to the control system. [Pause] What the mirror shows: [Pause] Zone 3-5 (hot center): Temperature uniformity ±3°C — if ΔT >5°C, induction rebalances before char quality drifts [Pause] CO/CO₂ ratio trajectory: The fingerprint of epoxy decomposition — if it lingers at 1.2 past 550°C, the ramp holds [Pause] BTX decay curve: The tar cracking progress — if m/z 91 (toluene) >10% at 650°C, steam injection increases [Pause] Char discharge rate: The mass balance closure — if Port 3 weigh belt drops below 0.3 kg/min, Port 3 actuator diagnostics trigger The mirror does not judge. [Pause] It reveals. [Pause] The control system judges. [Pause] The operator witnesses the judgment. [Pause] Self-reflection moment: At 680°C, the mirror asks: "Is this char ready for activation?" The proxies answer: CO/CO₂ decay rate <0.02/min, H₂/CH₄ >2.5, discharge steady. [Pause] If yes → Stage 4. [Pause] If no → 5 more minutes. [Pause] The forge knows its own readiness. [Pause]

Participation (Witness: How the Inside Operator Witnesses the Phenomenon into Existence)

The operator does not "run" Stage 3. [Pause] The operator witnesses it into existence. [Pause] At the HMI (Human-Machine Interface), the operator sees: [Pause] Thermal tomogram: 8-zone temperature map, updated 1 Hz, color-coded to ramp trajectory [Pause] Chemical tomogram: CO/CO₂ ratio trend, BTX decay, H₂ rise — live MS spectrum [Pause] Mass balance: Feed in (kg), char out (kg), gas out (Nm³), slag out (kg) — all live [Pause] Proxy dashboard: Green/amber/red for each quality hold point [Pause] Activation reactor: Bed temp, steam/CO₂ flow, burn-off estimate The operator's participation is threefold: [Pause] Category confirmation: Before batch start, operator verifies feedstock category (A/B/C/D) against PROD-13 assay. [Pause] This selects the DAEM parameter set. [Pause] Wrong category = wrong kinetics = failed batch. [Pause] The operator commits the identity of the feedstock. [Pause] [Pause] Hold point authorization: At each quality hold (500°C, 600°C, 680°C), the mirror shows proxies. [Pause] If amber (marginal), operator decides: extend 5 min or proceed? [Pause] The DAEM recommends; the operator authorizes. [Pause] This is the human-in-the-loop for irreducible uncertainty. [Pause] [Pause] Anomaly declaration: If the mirror shows red (off-spec: O₂ spike, vibration, seal leak, gas composition anomaly), the operator declares the anomaly. [Pause] This triggers interlock review, root cause logging, and batch disposition. [Pause] The operator owns the exception. [Pause] The witness is not passive. [Pause] The operator's category selection, hold authorization, and anomaly declaration are causal inputs to the batch outcome. [Pause] Without the operator's participation, the forge is a machine. [Pause] With it, the forge is a witnessed process. [Pause]

Protocol (Recursive: The Sequence That Guarantees Fidelity at Each Scale)

The recursive protocol: Measure → Heat → Separate → Verify. [Pause] At every scale. [Pause] Scale 1 — Microsecond (Control Loop): [Pause] Measure: FBG temp (1 kHz/zone), MS gas (1 Hz) [Pause] Heat: GaN inverter adjusts duty (1 kHz) [Pause] Separate: Induction coupling selects char over slag (σ difference) [Pause] Verify: Zone temp vs setpoint ±3°C, power vs limit Scale 2 — Minute (Ramp Segment): [Pause] Measure: CO/CO₂ ratio trajectory, BTX decay [Pause] Heat: Ramp generator advances setpoint (5°C/min) [Pause] Separate: Char forms, gas evolves, slag sinters [Pause] Verify: Hold point proxies vs thresholds Scale 3 — Batch (45 min Stage 3): [Pause] Measure: Total char yield, gas composition, slag mass [Pause] Heat: Integrated induction energy (48.75 kWh target) [Pause] Separate: Char → activation, Gas → PROD-07, Slag → Stage 7 [Pause] Verify: Mass balance closure 99.5-100.5%, quality specs met Scale 4 — Campaign (20 batches/category): [Pause] Measure: DAEM parameter drift, category consistency [Pause] Heat: Cumulative energy vs baseline [Pause] Separate: Activated char BET trend, Stage 1 adsorption life [Pause] Verify: Model update, category re-calibration Scale 5 — Lifetime (Forge service): [Pause] Measure: Drum liner wear, FBG drift, MS calibration [Pause] Heat: Refractory degradation, induction efficiency [Pause] Separate: Char quality vs activation energy [Pause] Verify: Major overhaul trigger, technology refresh The protocol recurses because the physics recurses. [Pause] Bond breaking (microsecond) → species evolution (second) → phase separation (minute) → product specification (batch) → process capability (campaign) → asset integrity (lifetime). [Pause] Each scale measures, heats, separates, verifies. [Pause] The output of one scale is the input to the next. [Pause] Fidelity guarantee: If any scale fails Verify, the protocol recurses downward to find the root cause. [Pause] Batch fail → check ramp segments → check control loops → check sensor calibration. [Pause] The recursion stops at the physical limit (sensor resolution, material property). [Pause] There is no "good enough" — only "verified at this scale." The operator's protocol: Witness the measure. [Pause] Authorize the heat. [Pause] Confirm the separate. [Pause] Sign the verify. [Pause] At every scale. [Pause] This is how the forge guarantees that what enters as mixed waste exits as separated elements — every batch, every campaign, every lifetime.

Combined Heuristic

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — Combined Heuristic Intro --- --- Three-Way Synthesis: Williams (Constraint) + Keymaker (Key) + El Segundo (Protocol) ---

Executive Synthesis

The pyrolysis window (450-700°C) is the thermodynamic gatekeeper of the entire cascade. [Pause] Williams identifies the constraint: thermosets cannot be melted, only decomposed — the 30-minute dwell at 5°C/min is physics, not policy. [Pause] Keymaker provides the key: real-time DAEM inversion using mass spec CO/CO₂ ratio closes the loop between first-principles kinetics and feedstock variability. [Pause] El Segundo mandates the protocol: Measure → Heat → Separate → Verify recurses across five scales (microsecond control loop → minute ramp segment → 45-minute batch → 20-batch campaign → forge lifetime), with the operator as causal witness at each hold point. [Pause] Three lenses, one conclusion: Stage 3 must be thermodynamically disciplined, computationally adaptive, and human-witnessed. [Pause] Any deviation — faster ramp, fixed schedule, unattended operation — breaks the cascade's REE partitioning, char quality, and energy recovery. [Pause]

[Pause: Long]

[Table Row: , Williams (Constraint) , O₂ allowed to 5% (air leak) , Nd/Fe/B oxidizes → REEs report to slag instead of Stage 7 metal → 90% Nd loss , MS: no direct O₂; slag REE assay post-batch , ]

[Table Row: , Keymaker (Key) , Fixed ramp schedule (no DAEM feedback) , Category B (high filler) under-pyrolyzes; Category D (pure thermoset) over-pyrolyzes → char BET 400-1000 m²/g swing , Activation reactor: burn-off 20-55% at 30 min , ]

[Table Row: , Keymaker (Key) , MS feedback disabled (sensor fault) , Ramp proceeds blind → tar >10 g/Nm³ → PROD-07 scrubber overload → dioxin risk , Post-batch tar gravimetric; PAH quarterly , ]

The three lenses are not redundant — they are orthogonal defenses. [Pause] Williams catches thermodynamic errors (ramp, atmosphere). [Pause] Keymaker catches kinetic errors (feedstock variability, sensor drift). [Pause] El Segundo catches human-system errors (authorization, anomaly response). [Pause] All three must hold for Stage 3 to deliver its three promises: clean REE partitioning, functional char, recoverable energy. [Pause]

[Pause] Together: The Aegis-Forge I Stage 3 is a thermodynamically rigorous, computationally self-correcting, human-witnessed pyrolysis reactor. [Pause] It transforms the liability of mixed thermoset waste into the assets of the cascade: energy gas, functional char, partitioned REEs, and a mineral slag ready for critical mineral recovery. [Pause] The three lenses agree — this is the only way to build a forge that separates elements from entropy.
